  ## Steps

  `scripts/create-app.mjs`.

  ## Message to the user
  Your React frontend is ready at `<target folder>`: Vite + React + TypeScript
  with ESLint. `npm run lint` is clean and `npm run build` passes.

  **What it does**
  - **Login (`/login`)** is the first screen. The username field only accepts an
  email address: it uses an email input, strips any spaces typed or pasted in,
  and checks the format and the 256-character limit before anything is sent. It
  signs in with `POST /api/auth/login`. Wrong credentials show "Invalid username
  or password." API validation errors appear on the matching field.
  - **Tasks (`/tasks`)** requires login. It's an empty page with your email and
  a **Sign out** button. The session is kept in sessionStorage and signs you out
  automatically when the token expires.

  **To run it**
  1. Start TaskManagement.Api with the **https** profile
  (`https://localhost:7213`).
  2. Run `npm run dev` in the app folder and open http://localhost:5173.

  **Heads-up on CORS:** the API allows only `https://localhost:5173`, but Vite
  serves plain `http`. To get around this, the dev server proxies `/api` to the
  API (see `vite.config.ts` / `.env.development`), so you don't need to change
  the API for local development. For a deployed build, set `VITE_API_BASE_URL`
  and add the UI's origin to the API's `Cors:AllowedOrigins`.

  **Logging in:** test users come from the API's `Auth:Users` configuration,
  which works in Development only. Check the API's user secrets or
  `appsettings.Development.json`.

  ## Message to the user
  The Task Management UI is set up in `<target folder>`. It uses Vite, React 19
  and TypeScript, linted with ESLint (typescript-eslint, react-hooks,
  react-refresh). `npm run lint` is clean and `npm run build` passes.

  **What's there**
  - **`/login`:** a username and password form. The username field only accepts
  an email address:
    - it is an email input,
    - spaces are removed as you type or paste,
    - it is checked against the `name@domain.tld` pattern and the API's
  256-character limit,
    - an invalid value blocks the request and shows an error under the field.

    The API's own validation errors are also shown on the matching fields.
  - **Login request:** it calls `POST /api/auth/login` on TaskManagement.Api
  (`AuthController`). The returned JWT is kept in sessionStorage, and you are
  signed out automatically when it expires. A wrong email and a wrong password
  both show "Invalid username or password.", so the page doesn't reveal which
  accounts exist.
  - **`/tasks`:** a placeholder page behind login, with a Sign out button.

  **To run it**
  1. Start TaskManagement.Api with the **https** profile
  (https://localhost:7213).
  2. In the app folder, run `npm run dev` and open http://localhost:5173.

  **CORS:** the API only allows `https://localhost:5173`, but Vite serves plain
  http. To get around this, the dev server proxies `/api` to the API
  (`API_PROXY_TARGET` in `.env.development`), so you don't need to change the
  API for local development. For a deployed build, set `VITE_API_BASE_URL` and
  add the UI's origin to the API's `Cors:AllowedOrigins`.

  **Logging in:** test users come from the API's `Auth:Users` configuration,
  which only works in the Development environment. They are in the API's user
  secrets or `appsettings.Development.json`. The UI contains no hard-coded
  credentials.

     - `POST /api/auth/login` with `{ username, password }`.
     - Username is required, at most 256 characters, and must be an email
  address.
     - Password is required and at most 128 characters.

       - Added `fieldsValid`: the username passes `validateUsername`, the
  password passes `validatePassword`, and no API field error is left unedited.
       - Added `canSubmit = fieldsValid && !submitting`. The button uses
  `disabled={!canSubmit}` and sets `aria-busy` while signing in.
       - Because the submit button is disabled, pressing Enter in a field does
  nothing.
       - `handleSubmit` keeps its validation guard as a backup and ignores a
  second submit while one is running.
       - Added a hint under the button ("Enter a valid email address and your
  password to sign in."), linked with `aria-describedby`. A disabled button
  can't be focused and gives no feedback on its own.
     - **`LoginPage.module.css`:** the disabled button uses `cursor:
  not-allowed` and lower opacity. While signing in (`aria-busy`) it keeps
  `cursor: progress`. Added a `.submitHint` style.

     - The rest of the template is unchanged: email-only username, whitespace
  stripping, errors shown after leaving a field, the Vite proxy, problem-details
  error mapping, and the expiring sessionStorage session.
  6. Re-ran `npm run lint` and `npm run build`. Both pass. No dev server was
  started.

  ## Message to the user
  The task management frontend is set up in
  `...\scratchpad\apps\disabled-submit-variant-with_skill`. It uses Vite 8,
  React 19 and TypeScript, with ESLint. `npm run lint` and `npm run build` both
  pass.

  **Login page:**
  - The username only accepts an email address. Spaces are removed as you type,
  and it must look like `name@domain.tld` (at most 256 characters).
  - The password must be 1–128 characters. Both limits match the API's
  `LoginCommandValidator`.
  - **Sign in stays disabled until both fields are valid**, and also while the
  request is being sent. A short hint under the button explains why it's
  disabled.
  - It sends `POST /api/auth/login` to AuthController. A 401 always shows
  "Invalid username or password.", and API validation errors appear on the
  matching field.
  - After you sign in, the token is kept in sessionStorage until it expires. You
  land on a placeholder `/tasks` page with a Sign out button.

  **To run it:** start TaskManagement.Api with the **https** profile
  (https://localhost:7213). Then run `npm run dev` and open
  http://localhost:5173.

  **CORS:** the API only allows `https://localhost:5173`, but Vite serves
  `http://`. In development, Vite forwards `/api` to the API (set by
  `API_PROXY_TARGET` in `.env.development`), so you don't need to change
  anything. For a deployed build, set `VITE_API_BASE_URL` and add the UI's
  address to the API's `Cors:AllowedOrigins`.

  **Test users** come from the API's `Auth:Users` setting and only work in
  Development. Look in the API's user secrets or `appsettings.Development.json`.

  **Autofill:** some browsers fill a saved password without telling the page
  until you interact with it. Sign in can then look disabled until you click or
  type in a field.

  # Summary: disabled-submit-variant (without skill)

  ## Steps

  1. **Read the API (read-only):**
     - `AuthController`: `POST /api/auth/login`.
     - `LoginRequest` and `LoginResponse` contracts.
     - `LoginCommandValidator`: username is required, at most 256 characters and
  an email; password is required, at most 128 characters.
     - `AuthErrors`: 401 returns "Invalid username or password."
     - Errors come back as ProblemDetails or ValidationProblemDetails.
     - `launchSettings.json`: https://localhost:7213 and http://localhost:5008.
     - `appsettings.json`: CORS allows only `https://localhost:5173`.
  2. **Node:** Node was not on the shell PATH, so I used `C:\Program
  Files\nodejs` (Node 24.21.0, npm 11.19.0).
  3. **Scaffold:** ran `npx create-vite@latest <dir> --template react-ts
  --no-interactive` (Vite 8, React 19, TS 6), then `npm install`.
  4. **Swapped oxlint for ESLint:** the current create-vite template ships
  oxlint, but ESLint was required.
     - Removed `oxlint` and `.oxlintrc.json`.
     - Installed `eslint`, `@eslint/js`, `typescript-eslint`,
  `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`, `eslint-plugin-react-refresh` and `globals`.
     - Added a flat `eslint.config.js` and set `"lint": "eslint ."`.
     - Checked that ESLint actually lints all the source files.
  5. **Configured `vite.config.ts`:**
     - Port 5173, with `strictPort`.
     - A dev proxy from `/api` to `https://localhost:7213`. It uses `secure:
  false` to accept the dev certificate and can be overridden with
  `VITE_API_PROXY_TARGET`.
     - The proxy avoids a CORS mismatch: the API allows
  `https://localhost:5173`, but Vite serves `http://localhost:5173`.
     - Added `.env.example` with `VITE_API_PROXY_TARGET` and
  `VITE_API_BASE_URL`.
  6. **Implemented:**
     - `src/api/auth.ts`: the `login()` client, plus an `ApiError` carrying the
  status and field errors parsed from ProblemDetails. Network failures produce a
  friendly message.
     - `src/auth/session.ts`: keeps the token in `sessionStorage`, checks
  expiry, and provides an `authorizationHeader()` helper for later task calls.
     - `src/pages/loginValidation.ts`: client-side rules that mirror
  `LoginCommandValidator`.
     - `src/pages/LoginPage.tsx` and `LoginPage.css`: the email and password
  form.
       - **Sign in is disabled until both fields are valid**, and also while a
  request is in flight.
       - Field errors show after the user leaves a field.
       - A 400 shows the server's field errors under the matching fields.
       - A 401 shows an alert and clears the password.
       - Has accessible labels, aria attributes and autocomplete hints.
     - `src/App.tsx`: shows the login page, or a signed-in placeholder with a
  Sign out button. It signs the user out when the token expires.
     - Light and dark styling through CSS variables.
     - Removed unused template assets, renamed the package to
  `task-management-ui`, and rewrote the README.
  7. **Checks:** ran `npm run lint` and `npm run build`; both pass. No dev

  ## Message to the user

  The Task Management UI is set up (Vite 8 + React 19 + TypeScript, ESLint) with
  the login page as the first screen.

  - **Login:** email + password, posted to `POST /api/auth/login` as `{
  username, password }`. Client-side rules match the API's
  `LoginCommandValidator` (valid email, max 256 chars; password required, max
  128).
  - **Your extra requirement:** the Sign in button stays disabled until both
  fields are valid, and also while the request is in flight so it can't be
  double-submitted.
  - **Errors:** a 401 shows "Invalid username or password." and clears the
  password. A 400 shows the API's field messages under the matching inputs. If
  the API can't be reached, you get a "Cannot reach the server" message.
  - **Session:** the JWT is kept in `sessionStorage`, and you're signed out
  automatically when it expires after 60 minutes. Use
  `authorizationHeader(session)` for task API calls.
  - **API connection:** in dev, Vite proxies `/api` to `https://localhost:7213`,
  so start the API with the `https` profile and run `npm run dev`. Without the
  proxy the login would fail CORS, because the API only allows
  `https://localhost:5173` and Vite serves plain `http://localhost:5173`. For a
  deployed build, set `VITE_API_BASE_URL` and add that UI origin to the API's
  `Cors:AllowedOrigins`.
  - **Lint and build:** `npm run lint` and `npm run build` both pass. I haven't
  tried a real sign-in against a running API, since no API or credentials were
  available.
  - **ESLint swap:** the current Vite template ships oxlint, so I replaced it
  with ESLint as you asked.

  ---- end summary.md content ----

  ## Intended content of outputs\summary.md

  # Summary – casual-with-tasks-page (without skill)

  ## Steps
  1. Read the API (read-only) to learn the auth contract:
     - `POST /api/auth/login`, body `{ username, password }`, returns `{
  accessToken, tokenType, expiresAtUtc, username }`.
     - Username must be an email (max 256); password max 128.
     - Errors are ProblemDetails with `code`, `detail` and `errors`. Bad
  credentials give 401 "Invalid username or password."
     - CORS only allows `https://localhost:5173`. The API's https profile is
  `https://localhost:7213`, and http on 5008 redirects to https.
     - The JWT lasts 60 minutes and there is no refresh token.
  2. Scaffolded with `npm create vite@latest -- --template react-ts`. The
  current template ships oxlint, so I removed it and set up ESLint (flat config:
  @eslint/js, typescript-eslint, react-hooks, react-refresh, globals) with
  `"lint": "eslint ."`.
  3. Added react-router-dom and @vitejs/plugin-basic-ssl. The dev server is
  pinned to `https://localhost:5173` (strictPort) to match the API's CORS
  origin. `VITE_API_BASE_URL` sets the API URL and defaults to
  `https://localhost:7213`; `.env.example` is included.
  4. Built the app:
     - `src/api/client.ts`: fetch wrapper that turns ProblemDetails into
  readable messages and reports when the API can't be reached.
     - `src/api/auth.ts`: `login()`.
     - `src/auth/*`: AuthProvider and useAuth (session in sessionStorage,
  automatic sign-out when the token expires), a RequireAuth guard, and email
  validation helpers.
     - `src/pages/LoginPage.tsx`: the email field strips whitespace as you type
  or paste and checks for a valid email. It shows an inline error, and the
  submit button stays disabled until the email is valid and a password is
  entered. Max lengths match the API, and API errors appear in an alert.
     - `src/pages/TasksPage.tsx`: protected, empty Tasks page with the user's
  email and a Log out button.
     - Routes: `/login`, `/tasks` (guarded), and everything else redirects to
  `/login`. Template boilerplate removed; light/dark CSS added.
  5. Ran lint and build, both clean. Smoke-tested the dev server (200 on
  `/login`), then stopped it. No real login was tested because the API wasn't
  running.

  ## Message to the user
  The React frontend is set up (Vite + React 19 + TypeScript, linted with
  ESLint). `npm run lint` and `npm run build` both pass with no errors.

  - **Login (first screen):** the username field only takes an email. Spaces are
  stripped, the email is checked, and **Sign in** stays disabled until the
  email is valid and a password is entered. It calls `POST /api/auth/login` and
  shows the API's error message (e.g. "Invalid username or password.").
  - **After login:** an empty Tasks page with your email in the header and a
  **Log out** button. `/tasks` is protected, so you're sent back to login if
  you're not signed in. You're signed out automatically when the 60-minute token
  expires.

  **How to run it:**
  1. Start the API with the **https** profile (`https://localhost:7213`).
  2. Run `npm install`, then `npm run dev`, and open **https://localhost:5173**.
  The dev server uses HTTPS on that exact port because the API's CORS only
  allows that origin. Accept the self-signed certificate warning once.
  3. If the API runs on a different URL, copy `.env.example` to `.env.local` and
  set `VITE_API_BASE_URL`.

  **Notes:**
  - The current Vite template ships oxlint instead of ESLint, so I replaced it
  with an ESLint flat config.
  - I couldn't test a real login because the API wasn't running. Your browser
  also has to trust the API's HTTPS dev certificate (`dotnet dev-certs https
  --trust`).

● Written for: you and whoever builds the UI next (or Claude in a later

  I created .claude/task.md. I named it task.md rather than taks.md to match the
  Api project's task.md, and it uses the same format: a decisions table, then
  work items with checkboxes.

  Your five requirements map to these work items:

  ┌─────────────────┬───────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Requirement   │ Work  │                   Approach                   │
  │                 │ item  │                                              │
  ├─────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ a. Display      │       │ Card grid (1–3+ columns depending on screen  │
  │ tasks           │ T2    │ width), with loading, empty and error states │
  │                 │       │  and pagination                              │
  ├─────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ b. Create a     │ T3    │ "New task" button opens a form dialog; field │
  │ task            │       │  checks match the API's rules                │
  ├─────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ c. Update       │ T4    │ Dropdown on each card; the card changes      │
  │ status          │       │ immediately and goes back if the save fails  │
  ├─────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │       │ Coloured left edge plus a badge with an icon │
  │ d. Priority     │ T5    │  and name for Low, Medium, High and          │
  │                 │       │ Critical, so colour isn't the only signal    │
  ├─────────────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ e. Clean look,  │ T6    │ CSS Modules with shared colour variables,    │
  │ no framework    │       │ visible keyboard focus, light and dark mode  │
  └─────────────────┴───────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Following your note about separate files, the "Folder layout" section splits
  the code under src/features/tasks/ into display/, create/, update/ and
  priority/. Two more files hold the API calls (taskApi.ts) and the list state
  (useTasks.ts).

  Things from your API that the plan accounts for:
  - Updating a status sends the whole task (D8). PUT /api/tasks/{id} replaces
    every field, so a status change must send the title, priority, due date and
    the rest too. Sending only status would fail the API's validation.
  - Paging: the list endpoint returns 20 tasks per page, so T2 includes
    Previous/Next controls. Without them, tasks after the first 20 can't be
    reached.
  - Status update errors: T4 says what to do when the API returns 404 (task
    deleted elsewhere) or 409 (changed by someone else).

  T7 covers lint, build and tests. T8 updates the skill so a regenerated UI
  includes the task board. Drag-and-drop status changes, edit and delete, and a
  summary header are in the backlog.

  Do you want me to start on T1?

> yes

● T1 is done. npm run lint and npm run build both pass, and I checked the date
  helpers with a short script: all 11 cases came out right.

  New files:
  - src/features/tasks/taskTypes.ts holds the task types, matched to the API:
    - TaskStatus and TaskPriority as lists of allowed values, since the
      project's TypeScript settings don't allow enum.
    - TaskDto, PagedResult, and the create and update request shapes.
    - Display labels (InProgress → "In progress") and the 200 / 4000 / 100 field
      limits.
  - src/features/tasks/taskApi.ts is now the only code that calls /api/tasks:
    - listTasks, createTask (returns the new id) and updateTask.
    - toUpdateRequest(task, changes) builds a full update body, because the
      API's update replaces the whole task. It sends the due date back
      unchanged, so you can still change the status of an overdue task.
    - isUnauthorized(error) spots an expired login.
  - src/lib/dates.ts holds shared date helpers. I moved parseUtc here from
    session.ts (src/auth/session.ts), which now imports it.

  How due dates work: a due date is a calendar day, sent as midnight UTC and
  always shown in UTC, so "20 Oct" reads the same in every time zone. The
  earliest date the form will offer is today in UTC, because that's what the API
  checks against. For users west of UTC in the evening, that can already be
  tomorrow.

  Still open: T1's last item, sending the user back to the login page when their
  session expires, is marked in progress in task.md (.claude/task.md). The
  check exists, but it only gets connected when the task-list logic is built in
  T2.

  Next is T2, which shows the tasks as cards on the Tasks page. Do you want me
  to go ahead?

> yes

● T2 is done, and lint and build pass. I haven't opened it in a browser against
  the running API yet, so that's left as an open item in task.md
  (.claude/task.md).

  After sign-in, the Tasks page now shows:
  - Header: app name, your email, Sign out.
  - Toolbar: "Tasks" heading, the task count, and Status and Priority filters.
    The API does the filtering, and changing a filter goes back to page 1.
  - Cards: 1 column on a phone and up to 3–4 on a wide screen. Each card shows
    the title, description, priority, assignee ("Unassigned" if empty) and due
    date. Past-due tasks that aren't done get an Overdue label. Done tasks have
    their title struck through and show the completion date.
  - Loading, errors, empty list:
    - Placeholder cards show on first load. When you change page or filter, the
      old cards stay, dimmed, until the new ones arrive.
    - An error banner has a Retry button.
    - An empty list has a message, with Clear filters if filters are hiding
      everything.
  - Pagination: Previous/Next with "Page X of Y", shown only when there's more
    than one page.

  If your login expires, any task request now sends you back to the login page.
  That completes the last open item in T1.

  All of the files are separate, as you asked:
  - useTasks.ts (src/features/tasks/useTasks.ts) loads the tasks and holds the
    list state.
  - taskErrors.ts (src/features/tasks/taskErrors.ts) turns API errors into
    readable messages, and will be reused in T3 and T4.
  - display/ (src/features/tasks/display/) has TaskCard, TaskList, EmptyState,
    TaskFilters and Pagination, each with its own stylesheet.
  - TasksPage.tsx (src/pages/TasksPage.tsx) puts them together.

  I added a few colour variables for cards and loading placeholders to index.css
  (src/index.css), in light and dark versions.

  On the cards, status is a grey label and priority is plain text for now. T4
  turns status into the dropdown and T5 adds the coloured priority badge. The
  New task button comes in T3 with its form.

  To see it, start the API with the https profile, run npm run dev, and sign in
  at http://localhost:5173.

  Should I continue with T3, the create-task form?

● I updated .claude/task.md to use a table instead of cards, with the Status and
  Priority filters in the column headers. I only changed the plan; the code
  still shows cards until the table is built.

  Decisions:
  - D2 now says table layout, dated 2026-10-11. The columns are Title (with the
    description underneath), Status, Priority, Assigned to and Due date.
  - D9 (new): the Status and Priority column headers each have a filter dropdown
    with "All" plus each value. The API does the filtering, so it covers every
    page, not just the 20 visible rows. When nothing matches, the header row
    stays, so the filters can always be changed back.
  - D10 (new): on small screens the table scrolls sideways inside its own box,
    with the Title column fixed on the left. The page itself never scrolls
    sideways.

  T2 is back to in progress ([~]):
  - Still done: the list-loading logic, error messages, pagination and empty
    state carry over unchanged.
  - New items: the table, its loading and error states, the column filters, and
    a full-width "No tasks match these filters" row with a Clear filters button.
    Optionally, the filters can be kept in the URL so a refresh or shared link
    keeps them.
  - Cleanup: the card files (TaskCard, TaskList, TaskFilters) are listed for
    deletion. The folder layout now shows TaskTable, TaskRow and ColumnFilter in
    their place.

  The later tasks now say "row" instead of "card". The changes that matter most:
  - T4: if you change a task's status while a Status filter is active, the row
    stays visible until the next reload, so it doesn't vanish under your cursor.
  - T5: the priority colour bar sits on each row's left edge, and the Priority
    filter's options match the badges.
  - T3: if filters would hide a newly created task, they're cleared so you see
    it.

  I also added backlog item T12, sorting by column. The API always returns tasks
  newest first and has no sort option, so sorting only the visible page would
  be misleading. It needs an API change first.

  Should I build the table version of T2 now?

> yes

● The Tasks page now shows a table instead of cards, with the Status and
  Priority filters in their column headers. npm run lint and npm run build both
  pass. I haven't checked it in a browser against the running API yet, so how it
  looks, including at phone width, is still unconfirmed.

  What you'll see
  - Columns: Title (with a one-line description underneath), Status, Priority,
    Assigned to and Due date. Long text is cut off with "…", and the full text
    shows when you hover.
  - Filters: the Status and Priority headers each have a dropdown starting at
    "All". A filter in use is highlighted in the accent colour, and the toolbar
    then shows Clear filters and reads "N tasks matching filters". The API
    applies the filters across all your tasks, not just the 20 on screen, and a
    filter change goes back to page 1.
  - No matches: the header row and filters stay visible, and a single row says
    "No tasks match these filters" with a Clear filters button. If you have no
    tasks at all, the "No tasks yet" message shows instead of the table.
  - Rows: done tasks are greyed out with the title struck through and a
    "Completed {date}" in the Due date column. Late tasks get a red Overdue
    label.
  - Loading:
    - The first load shows placeholder rows under the real header.
    - When you change page or filter, the old rows stay, dimmed, until the new
      ones arrive.
    - An error banner with a Retry button shows above the table.
  - Paging: "Showing 21–40 of 57" is always shown, and Previous/Next appear when
    there's more than one page.
  - Small screens: only the table scrolls sideways, and the Title column stays
    fixed on the left.

  Files (in src/features/tasks/display/):
  - New: TaskTable.tsx (src/features/tasks/display/TaskTable.tsx), TaskRow.tsx
    (src/features/tasks/display/TaskRow.tsx) and ColumnFilter.tsx
    (src/features/tasks/display/ColumnFilter.tsx), each with its own stylesheet.
    One filter component serves both columns.
  - Updated: Pagination.tsx (src/features/tasks/display/Pagination.tsx),
    EmptyState.tsx (src/features/tasks/display/EmptyState.tsx), TasksPage.tsx
    (src/pages/TasksPage.tsx), and useTasks.ts (src/features/tasks/useTasks.ts),
    which now also returns pageSize.
  - Deleted: the card version's TaskCard, TaskList and TaskFilters.

  Still open in T2 (marked in task.md (.claude/task.md)):
  - The browser check, including phone width.
  - The New task button, which comes with T3.
  - Optionally, keeping the filters in the page URL so a refresh or shared link
    keeps them.

  To try it, start the API with the https profile, run npm run dev, and sign in
  at http://localhost:5173.

● I'll add this to task.md (.claude/task.md) as a plan change, without changing
  any code. T4 already covers changing a row's status, so I'll widen it to cover
  priority as well rather than start a separate item. Status and priority save
  through the same API call, so handling them together keeps the two from
  conflicting on the same row.

● I added this to task.md (.claude/task.md) by widening T4 to "Update status and
  priority on each row, saved to the database" rather than adding a separate
  item. Status and priority are saved by the same API call, so handling them
  together stops two changes to the same row from overwriting each other. I only
  changed the plan; no code has changed yet.

  How it will work
  - Each row gets two dropdowns: Status (To do / In progress / Done) and
    Priority (Low / Medium / High / Critical). Choosing a value saves it
    straight away; there's no Save button.
  - The change is sent with PUT /api/tasks/{id}. The API saves it to the Tasks
    table in SQL Server and updates ModifiedDate. It also sets or clears
    CompletedAtUtc when a task moves to or from Done.
  - The full task is always sent, so changing the priority never resets the
    status, title or due date.
  - New rule D11: a change only shows as saved once the API confirms it. The row
    then reloads from the database with GET /api/tasks/{id}. If the save fails,
    the row goes back to its saved values and shows the error, so nothing looks
    saved when it wasn't.
  - While a row is saving, both of its dropdowns are disabled, and the cell
    shows "Saving…" then "Saved". Other rows stay editable.
  - The priority dropdown keeps the priority icon and colours from T5, so the
    colour coding still shows while it's editable.

  Checking it's in the database: T4 now ends with a manual check:
  - change a row's status, then its priority,
  - reload the page and confirm both changes are still there,
  - run SELECT Title, Status, Priority, ModifiedDate, CompletedAtUtc FROM Tasks
    WHERE Id = '…' against LocalDB. Status and Priority are stored as text, so
    they should match what you picked.
  - turn the API off, change a row, and confirm it reverts and shows an error.

  I also updated the related sections:
  - D3, the folder layout and the API table now list PrioritySelect.tsx,
    useRowUpdate.ts and getTask().
  - T5 reuses the same icons and colours in the priority dropdown.
  - T7 adds tests that a priority change sends the unchanged status and title,
    and that the row reloads after saving. Its smoke test now includes a page
    reload.
  - T8 mentions the new rules for the skill.

  Do you want me to build T4 now, or do T3 (the create-task form) first as the
  list suggests?

> Implement the T3 for create-task and create T4 also to update the priority
  and status

  the "Saved" tick:

       74    const reload = useCallback(() => setReloadCount((count) => c
           ount + 1), [])
       75  
       76 +  const replaceTask = useCallback((task: TaskDto) => {        
       77 +    setData((current) =>                                      
       78 +      current && { ...current, items: current.items.map((item)
          + => (item.id === task.id ? task : item)) },                   
       79 +    )                                                         
       80 +  }, [])                                                      
       81 +                                                              
       82 +  const removeTask = useCallback((id: string) => {            
       83 +    setData((current) =>                                      
       84 +      current && {                                            
       85 +        ...current,                                           
       86 +        items: current.items.filter((item) => item.id !== id),
       87 +        totalCount: Math.max(current.totalCount - 1, 0),      
       88 +      },                                                      
       89 +    )                                                         
       90 +  }, [])                                                      
       91 +                                                              
       92 +  /**                                                         
       93 +   * T3: creates a task, then shows page 1 (newest first) so t
          +he new task is at the top.                                    
       94 +   * Filters are cleared too, in case they would hide it. Thro
          +ws on failure so the form can show the errors.                
       95 +   */                                                         
       96 +  const createTask = useCallback(                             
       97 +    async (request: CreateTaskRequest): Promise<string> => {  
       98 +      if (!token) throw new Error('Not signed in.')           
       99 +      try {                                                   
      100 +        const id = await taskApi.createTask(request, token)   
      101 +        setFiltersState({})                                   
      102 +        setPage(1)                                            
      103 +        reload()                                              
      104 +        return id                                             
      105 +      } catch (error) {                                       
      106 +        if (taskApi.isUnauthorized(error)) logout()           
      107 +        throw error                                           
      108 +      }                                                       
      109 +    },                                                        
      110 +    [token, logout, reload],                                  
      111 +  )                                                           
      112 +                                                              
      113 +  /**                                                         
      114 +   * T4: saves a status or priority change for one row to the 
          +database (D3, D11).                                           
      115 +   * 1. shows the change straight away, 2. PUTs the whole task
          + (D8), 3. on 204 replaces the row with the                    
      116 +   * database's copy. On failure the row goes back to its save
          +d values.                                                     
      117 +   * Resolves to null when saved, or to an error message for t
          +he row.                                                       
      118 +   */                                                         
      119 +  const updateTaskFields = useCallback(                       
      120 +    async (task: TaskDto, changes: TaskFieldChanges): Promise<
          +string | null> => {                                           
      121 +      if (!token) return 'Not signed in.'                     
      122 +      replaceTask({ ...task, ...changes })                    
      123 +                                                              
      124 +      try {                                                   
      125 +        await taskApi.updateTask(task.id, taskApi.toUpdateRequ
          +est(task, changes), token)                                    
      126 +      } catch (error) {                                       
      127 +        if (taskApi.isUnauthorized(error)) {                  
      128 +          logout()                                            
      129 +          return 'Your session has expired.'                  
      130 +        }                                                     
      131 +        if (error instanceof ApiError && error.status === 404)
          + {                                                            
      132 +          removeTask(task.id)                                 
      133 +          setNotice(`"${task.title}" no longer exists. It may 
          +have been deleted by someone else.`)                          
      134 +          return null                                         
      135 +        }                                                     
      136 +        replaceTask(task) // revert to the last saved values  
      137 +        if (error instanceof ApiError && error.status === 409)
          + {                                                            
      138 +          setNotice('A task was changed by someone else at the
          + same time. The list has been refreshed; please try again.')  
      139 +          reload()                                            
      140 +          return 'Not saved: changed by someone else.'        
      141 +        }                                                     
      142 +        return `Not saved. ${describeTaskError(error)}`       
      143 +      }                                                       
      144 +                                                              
      145 +      // Saved. Show exactly what the database now holds (Modi
          +fiedDate, CompletedAtUtc, ...).                               
      146 +      try {                                                   
      147 +        replaceTask(await taskApi.getTask(task.id, token))    
      148 +      } catch {                                               
      149 +        // The save itself succeeded; keep the optimistic row 
          +until the next reload.                                        
      150 +      }                                                       
      151 +      return null                                             
      152 +    },                                                        
      153 +    [token, logout, replaceTask, removeTask, reload],         
      154 +  )                                                           
      155 +                                                              
      156    return {
      157      tasks: data?.items ?? [],
      158      totalCount: data?.totalCount ?? 0,
     ...
      169      /** False until the first page has loaded at least once. *
           /
      170      hasLoaded: data !== null,
      171      reload,
      172 +    createTask,                                               
      173 +    updateTaskFields,                                         
      174 +    notice,                                                   
      175 +    dismissNotice: useCallback(() => setNotice(null), []),    
      176    }
      177  }

           s.updateTaskFields()` → `PUT /api/tasks/{id}` with the full ta

● T3 (create a task) and T4 (change status and priority on each row) are built,
  and npm run lint and npm run build both pass. I haven't confirmed that changes
  reach the database: the API isn't running, and I don't have the dev user's
  password. Only its hash is stored, and I didn't create a new user because that
  would change your API's secrets.

  T3, New task (in create/ (src/features/tasks/create/))
  - A New task button in the toolbar, and in the "No tasks yet" message, opens a
    dialog with these fields:
    - Title (required)
    - Description (with a character counter)
    - Priority (defaults to Medium)
    - Assigned to
    - Due date (no dates before today)
  - The checks match the API's rules. Errors appear after you leave a field or
    press Create, and the cursor jumps to the first problem.
  - When you press Create:
    - the API's own field errors show on the matching fields,
    - if the request fails, everything you typed stays in the form,
    - on success the dialog closes, any filters are cleared, and the new task
      appears at the top of the table.

  T4, change status and priority (in update/ (src/features/tasks/update/))
  - Each row now has a Status dropdown and a Priority dropdown. Picking a value
    saves it straight away through PUT /api/tasks/{id}. The API then saves it to
    the Tasks table and updates ModifiedDate, plus CompletedAtUtc when the task
    moves to or from Done.
  - The whole task is sent with each change, so a priority change never resets
    the status or anything else.
  - Saving: a spinner shows while it saves, then a green tick once the API
    confirms it. The row then reloads from the database, so it shows exactly
    what's stored.
  - While saving: both dropdowns in that row are locked, so two saves can't
    overwrite each other. Other rows stay editable.
  - If saving fails: the row goes back to its saved values and shows the reason
    in the cell. If the task was deleted elsewhere or changed by someone else at
    the same time, a banner above the table says so. If your login has expired,
    you're signed out.
  - Filters: if a change means a row no longer matches the active filter, the
    row stays visible with the note "Hidden by the current filter after
    refresh", so it doesn't vanish under your cursor.

  Other changes
  - useTasks.ts (src/features/tasks/useTasks.ts) now has createTask and
    updateTaskFields.
  - taskApi.ts (src/features/tasks/taskApi.ts) has a new getTask call.
  - TaskRow.tsx (src/features/tasks/display/TaskRow.tsx), TaskTable.tsx
    (src/features/tasks/display/TaskTable.tsx) and TasksPage.tsx
    (src/pages/TasksPage.tsx) are updated to use them.
  - The table's minimum width went from 760px to 920px to fit the two dropdowns.
  - task.md (.claude/task.md) has T3 and T4 ticked off. Both stay "in progress"
    until the checks below pass.

          -11). One row per task, with these columns: **Title**, **Status
          **, **Priority**, **Assigned to**, **Due date**. A table is eas
          ier to scan and compare across many tasks, and gives filters a 
          natural home in the column headers (D9). |

          pdown** (`<select>`) in its **Status** cell (To do, In progress
          , Done) and in its **Priority** cell (Low, Medium, High, Critic
          al). Changing either one saves straight away to the database th
          rough `PUT /api/tasks/{id}`; there is no separate Save button. 
          Dropdowns work with the keyboard and screen readers, and need n
          o drag-and-drop library. Drag-and-drop is a possible later work
           item (T9). Added priority on 2026-10-11. |
      18  | D4 | Priority display | A coloured 4px bar on the row's left 
          edge, plus a badge with an icon and the priority's name in the

         +they were chosen for. No CSS framework. |

           refresh). No extra data library for now. |

  background and on white, lowest 5.39:1 against the 4.5:1 standard)

  ┌─────────────┬────────┬─────┬──────────┬────────┬──────┐
  │   Status    │ Colour │     │ Priority │ Colour │ Icon │

  │ In progress │ blue   │     │ Medium   │ violet │ =    │

  │ Done        │ green  │     │ High     │ orange │ ↑    │
  ├─────────────┼────────┼─────┼──────────┼────────┼──────┤
  │             │        │     │ Critical │ red    │ ⚠    │
  └─────────────┴────────┴─────┴──────────┴────────┴──────┘

  - Medium is violet rather than blue so it can't be mistaken for In progress.

    coloured text. The open list stays plain black on white, so every option is
    easy to read.
  - Each row has a 4px bar in its priority colour on the left edge. It stays
    visible when the table scrolls sideways on small screens.
  - Critical rows get a faint red background so they stand out.
  - When you change a priority, the colour, icon and bar update immediately.

      106  ## Why the template is built this way
      107  
      108  Keep these properties when you adapt or extend the app. Each o
           ne exists for a reason.
      109  
      110 +### Login                                                     
      111 +                                                              
      112  **The username accepts only an email address.** This is the us
           er's core requirement, so it is
      113  enforced in layers. No single layer is enough:
      114  - `type="email"` + `inputMode="email"` + `autoComplete="userna
           me"` give mobile users the email
      115    keyboard and let password managers fill the field.
      116  - `onChange` strips all whitespace (`sanitizeEmailInput`), so 
           spaces can't be typed or pasted in.
      117  - `validateUsername` checks a `local@domain.tld` pattern and t
           he 256-character limit. Errors show
       97 -  after blur or the first submit attempt, not on the first key
          -stroke. Submitting with an invalid value                      
       98 -  focuses the field and sends no request.                     
      118 +  after blur or the first submit attempt, not on the first key
          +stroke.                                                       
      119  - `noValidate` on the form turns off the browser's inconsisten
           t built-in bubbles, so our messages are
      120    the only ones shown and they are announced via `aria-describ
           edby`.
      121  - The API's FluentValidation `EmailAddress()` is the last line
            of defence. Its 400 `errors.Username`
      122    is mapped back onto the field.
      123  
      124 +**A 401 at login always says "Invalid username or password."**
          +, for an unknown email or a wrong                             
      125 +password, so the UI never reveals which accounts exist.       
      126 +                                                              
      127 +**The session lives in sessionStorage and expires.** `expiresA
          +tUtc` drives an automatic sign-out                            
      128 +timer, and any 401 from a task call signs out too (handled onc
          +e, in `useTasks`). sessionStorage                             
      129 +survives a refresh but not closing the tab, which is a reasona
          +ble default for a JWT readable from JS.                       
      130 +Don't switch to localStorage unless the user asks.            
      131 +                                                              
      132 +### Talking to the API                                        
      133 +                                                              
      134  **API calls go through a Vite proxy.** The API's CORS allows o
           nly `https://localhost:5173`, but Vite
      135  serves `http://`. Proxying `/api` to `API_PROXY_TARGET` keeps 
           requests same-origin in development, so
      136  the user doesn't have to touch the API. For a deployed build, 
           set `VITE_API_BASE_URL` and add the UI's
      137  origin to the API's `Cors:AllowedOrigins`.
      138  
      139  **Errors come from problem details.** Every API error is `appl
           ication/problem+json` with a stable
      110 -`code`. `loginErrors.ts` switches on `code`/status, not on Eng
          -lish text. A 401 always shows the same                        
      111 -"Invalid username or password." for an unknown email or a wron
          -g password, so the UI never reveals which                     
      112 -accounts exist.                                               
      140 +`code` and, on 400, field `errors` keyed C#-style (`Title`, `D
          +ueDateUtc`). Map on `code`/status and                         
      141 +field keys, never on English text.                            
      142  
      114 -**The session lives in sessionStorage and expires.** `expiresA
          -tUtc` from the login response drives an                       
      115 -automatic sign-out timer. `.NET` can omit the trailing `Z`, so
          - `parseUtc` adds it. sessionStorage                           
      116 -survives a refresh but not closing the tab, which is a reasona
          -ble default for a JWT readable from JS.                       
      117 -Don't switch to localStorage unless the user asks.            
      143 +**`taskApi.ts` is the only code that calls `/api/tasks`.** Com
          +ponents and hooks go through it.                              
      144  
      119 -**The files are split to satisfy the generated ESLint config.*
          -* `eslint-plugin-react-refresh` flags                         
      120 -files that export both components and non-components, so the c
          -ontext object (`AuthContext.ts`), the                         
      121 -provider (`AuthProvider.tsx`) and the hook (`useAuth.ts`) are 
          -separate files. The tsconfig has                              
      122 -`verbatimModuleSyntax` (use `import type`) and `erasableSyntax
          -Only` (no `enum`, no constructor                              
      123 -parameter properties). Follow the same rules in any code you a
          -dd, or `npm run build` fails.                                 
      145 +### Task table                                                
      146  
      147 +**A table, with the Status and Priority filters in the column 
          +headers.** The user chose a table over                        
      148 +cards. The API does the filtering (`?status=&priority=`), so a
          + filter covers every page, not just the                       
      149 +visible 20. A filter change goes back to page 1. When filters 
          +match nothing, the header row stays,                          
      150 +with a "No tasks match these filters" row, so the user can alw
          +ays change the filters back. On narrow                        
      151 +screens the table scrolls sideways inside its own box (`min-wi
          +dth` 920px) and the Title column is sticky.                   
      152 +                                                              
      153 +**Responses are tagged with the page and filters they were for
          +** (`useTasks`), so a slow, outdated                          
      154 +response can't overwrite a newer one. The previous rows stay, 
          +dimmed, while the next page loads.                            
      155 +                                                              
      156 +### Creating tasks                                            
      157 +                                                              
      158 +**The form mirrors `CreateTaskCommandValidator`:** title requi
          +red (≤ 200 after trimming), description                       
      159 +≤ 4000, assigned-to ≤ 100, due date not before today. "Today" 
          +is the **UTC** day, because that is what                      
      160 +the API checks. Due dates are calendar days sent as `YYYY-MM-D
          +DT00:00:00Z` and shown in UTC, so they                        
      161 +read the same in every time zone. After creating, the filters 
          +are cleared and page 1 reloads, so the new                    
      162 +task is visible at the top. A failed request keeps everything 
          +the user typed.                                               
      163 +                                                              
      164 +### Updating status and priority                              
      165 +                                                              
      166 +**`PUT /api/tasks/{id}` replaces the whole task.** Always buil
          +d the body with `toUpdateRequest(task,                        
      167 +changes)`, which copies every field. Sending only `{ status }`
          + fails validation; forgetting a field                         
      168 +would wipe it. Send `dueDateUtc` back exactly as received: the
          + API only rejects a past due date when it                     
      169 +*changes*, so overdue tasks stay editable.                    
      170 +                                                              
      171 +**"Saved" means the database has it.** A row change shows stra
          +ight away, but it is marked saving until                      
      172 +the API answers 204. The row is then replaced with `GET /api/t
          +asks/{id}`, so it shows what SQL Server                       
      173 +holds (`ModifiedDate`, `CompletedAtUtc`). On failure the row r
          +everts and the cell says why: 404 removes                     
      174 +the row with a notice, 409 reloads the list.                  
      175 +                                                              
      176 +**One save per row at a time.** Both dropdowns in a row are di
          +sabled while it saves, because two                            
      177 +overlapping full-object PUTs could undo each other. Other rows
          + stay editable. A row that no longer                          
      178 +matches the active filter after an edit stays visible, with a 
          +note, until the next reload, so it doesn't                    
      179 +vanish under the cursor.                                      
      180 +                                                              
      181 +### Look                                                      
      182 +                                                              
      183 +**Light theme only.** The user asked for it, so there is no `p
          +refers-color-scheme: dark` block and                          
      184 +`color-scheme` is `light`. Don't add dark mode back unless ask
          +ed. The status and priority colours were                      
      185 +chosen for light backgrounds.                                 
      186 +                                                              
      187 +**Status and priority are colour-coded, and colour is never th
          +e only signal.**                                              
      188 +                                                              
      189 +| Status | Colour | | Priority | Colour | Icon |              
      190 +|---|---|---|---|---|---|                                     
      191 +| To do | grey | | Low | grey | ↓ |                           
      192 +| In progress | blue | | Medium | violet | = |                
      193 +| Done | green | | High | orange | ↑ |                        
      194 +| | | | Critical | red, plus a row tint | ⚠ |                 
      195 +                                                              
      196 +- Each value has `-fg` / `-bg` / `-border` tokens in `index.cs
          +s`, and every `-fg` meets WCAG AA (≥ 4.5:1)                   
      197 +  on its `-bg` and on white. Re-check contrast if you change a
          + colour.                                                      
      198 +- The dropdowns are tinted through `data-status` / `data-prior
          +ity` in `update/rowSelect.module.css`.                        
      199 +- Each row's priority bar is an inset shadow on the sticky Tit
          +le cell, keyed on `data-priority` on the                      
      200 +  `<tr>`.                                                     
      201 +- Medium is violet, not blue, so it can't be confused with In 
          +progress.                                                     
      202 +                                                              
      203 +**No CSS framework.** Use CSS Modules and the tokens in `index
          +.css`; don't hard-code colours in                             
      204 +components.                                                   
      205 +                                                              
      206 +### Code shape                                                
      207 +                                                              
      208 +**One folder per job, one job per file:** `display/`, `create/
          +`, `update/` and `priority/` under                            
      209 +`features/tasks/`. The user asked for this split; don't merge 
          +features back into one big file.                              
      210 +                                                              
      211 +**The files are also split to satisfy the generated ESLint con
          +fig.** `eslint-plugin-react-refresh`                          
      212 +flags files that export both components and non-components. Th
          +at is why the context object                                  
      213 +(`AuthContext.ts`), the provider (`AuthProvider.tsx`) and the 
          +hook (`useAuth.ts`) are separate files,                       
      214 +and why helpers live in `.ts` files apart from components.    
      215 +                                                              
      216 +The tsconfig has `verbatimModuleSyntax` (use `import type`) an
          +d `erasableSyntaxOnly` (no `enum`; use                        
      217 +string-literal unions such as `TASK_STATUSES`). `eslint-plugin
          +-react-hooks` v7 rejects synchronous                          
      218 +`setState` inside effects; set state in async callbacks, or de
          +rive it, as `useTasks` does with its                          
      219 +`loading` flag. Follow the same rules in any code you add, or 
          +lint or build fails.                                          
      220 +                                                              
      221  ## If the script can't be used

      234 +- **API contract details** (endpoints, fields, limits, error c
          +odes, ports, CORS): read                                      
      235 +  `references/api-contract.md`. If the user says the API chang
          +ed, re-read the C# files it points to                         
      236 +  rather than trusting the doc.

          +uest(path, { token })`. Route errors through                  
      238 +  `describeTaskError`, and let `useTasks` handle 401.         
      239 +- **Editing more fields inline:** extend `TaskFieldChanges` an
          +d add a select or input beside the existing                   
      240 +  ones. It saves through the same `updateTaskFields` path, so 
          +it inherits revert, refresh and row locking.                  
      241 +- **Sorting:** the API has no sort parameter and always return
          +s newest first. Add one to the API before                     
      242 +  offering column sorting; sorting only the visible page would
          + be misleading.

       70  | 401    | `Auth.Unauthorized`        | Missing or expired tok

       72  
       73 +## Tasks (Controllers/TasksController.cs), all need `Authoriza
          +tion: Bearer {accessToken}`                                   
       74 +                                                              
       75 +| Operation | Method & route | Success | Errors |             
       76 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
       77 +| List | `GET /api/tasks?status=&priority=&page=1&pageSize=20`
          + | 200 `PagedResult<TaskDto>` | 400, 401 |                    
       78 +| Summary | `GET /api/tasks/summary` | 200 | 401, 500 |       
       79 +| Get one | `GET /api/tasks/{id}` | 200 `TaskDto` | 401, 404 |
       80 +| Create | `POST /api/tasks` | 201 `{ id }` + `Location` | 400
          +, 401 |                                                       
       81 +| Update | `PUT /api/tasks/{id}` | 204 | 400, 401, 404, 409 | 
       82 +| Soft delete | `DELETE /api/tasks/{id}` | 204 | 401, 404 |   
       83 +                                                              
       84 +**TaskDto** (`Application/Tasks/TaskDto.cs`):                 
       85 +                                                              
       86 +```json                                                       
       87 +{                                                             
       88 +  "id": "guid", "title": "…", "description": "…|null", "status
          +": "ToDo|InProgress|Done",                                    
       89 +  "priority": "Low|Medium|High|Critical", "assignedTo": "…|nul
          +l", "dueDateUtc": "ISO|null",                                 
       90 +  "createdDate": "ISO", "modifiedDate": "ISO|null", "completed
          +AtUtc": "ISO|null"                                            
       91 +}                                                             
       92 +```                                                           
       93 +                                                              
       94 +**PagedResult:** `items`, `page`, `pageSize`, `totalCount`, `t
          +otalPages`. `pageSize` is 1–100                               
       95 +(`ListTasksQueryValidator`), and results come newest first. Th
          +ere is **no sort parameter**.                                 
       96 +                                                              
       97 +**Enums** travel as names (`"InProgress"`), never numbers. The
          + JSON converter disallows integers.                           
       98 +`TaskItemConfiguration` stores them as text in SQL, so the dat
          +abase shows the same names.                                   
       99 +                                                              
      100 +**Create** (`CreateTaskRequest`): `title`, `description`, `pri
          +ority`, `assignedTo`, `dueDateUtc`. New tasks                 
      101 +always start as `ToDo`. The validator (`CreateTaskCommandValid
          +ator`, `TaskValidationRules`) checks:                         
      102 +- `title`: required, ≤ 200 (`TaskItem.TitleMaxLength`),       
      103 +- `description`: ≤ 4000,                                      
      104 +- `assignedTo`: ≤ 100,                                        
      105 +- `priority`: required, a valid name,                         
      106 +- `dueDateUtc`: not before the current **UTC** date.          
      107 +                                                              
      108 +**Update** (`UpdateTaskRequest`): `title`, `description`, `sta
          +tus`, `priority`, `assignedTo`, `dueDateUtc`.                 
      109 +**A full replacement:** every field must be sent. `status` and
          + `priority` are required, so a partial                        
      110 +body fails with 400.                                          
      111 +- The update validator has no due-date rule. The domain (`Task
          +Item.SetDetails`) only rejects a past due                     
      112 +  date when the value **changes**, so send the stored `dueDate
          +Utc` back unchanged.                                          
      113 +- The domain sets `ModifiedDate` on every update, and sets or 
          +clears `CompletedAtUtc` when the status                       
      114 +  moves to or from `Done`.                                    
      115 +- 409 comes from the `RowVersion` concurrency token. It isn't 
          +exposed in the DTO, so it only fires when                     
      116 +  two saves race on the server.                               
      117 +                                                              
      118 +**Database:** the `Tasks` table in `TaskManagementDb` (LocalDB
          + in development), soft-deleted rows filtered                  
      119 +out by a global query filter. To confirm a UI change was saved
          +:                                                             
      120 +                                                              
      121 +```sql                                                        
      122 +SELECT Title, Status, Priority, ModifiedDate, CompletedAtUtc F
          +ROM Tasks ORDER BY ModifiedDate DESC;                         
      123 +```                                                           
      124 +

           e/Auth/ConfiguredUserAuthenticator.cs`)
